# Matched-runtime three-arm diagnostic

The fresh complete attempt passes every timing, completion, physical and matched-setting gate. It is one screening pass, not repeated benchmark medians or a new supported recommendation. The earlier attempt is discarded as a comparison: only its original arm ran; the matched arm was refused before loading after the existing continuous-quiet admission deadline. Both attempts and the prospectively frozen single fresh attempt are preserved verbatim below. No partial subset or replacement cell contributes to the complete comparison.

Both matched arms use the same verification, forecast, prefill and context settings, with original four-bit versus native three-bit expert records and their resulting different cache capacities. Their authenticated physical loaders remain different. Thus this separates runtime recipe from the full quantization/deployment benefit; it does not identify a pure kernel-speed effect or individually isolate verification, forecast and reserve costs. The coded natural answer is identical in all three arms. All results remain below twenty committed generation tokens per second.

The preregistered runtime-overhead screening criterion passes. Target verification dominates the measured time, and the uncorrected matched forecast reads more unused bytes. This justifies one next prospective two-arm candidate correction test: same exact candidate, arithmetic, work and safety conditions, uncorrected versus explicitly pinned original correction, with correction memory charged. Exact committed tokens/text must remain unchanged. Require at least five-percent generation improvement on both fixed workloads and no more than five-percent coding response-time regression; otherwise stop this transfer recipe. No retuning against these answers, relaxed quality gates or automatic product activation follows.
